It just comes out that way. And people... they just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying at all. It's making it really hard to do my job, so I figured I needed to get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear sounds or voices that other people around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or sounds that aren't there. It's really just my own words getting all jumbled up when I try to talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually energized, irritable, or perhaps significantly down or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel pretty normal as far as my mood goes. I haven't had any big bursts of energy or felt super irritable or anything like that. And I haven't been feeling down or hopeless either. It's just... frustrating, you know? The main thing is just my speech getting all mixed up at work. But mood-wise, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol or recreational drugs recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't use any drugs at all. And as for alcohol... I mean, I might have a beer with dinner once in a blue moon, but nothing recently. It's definitely not that. I've just been going to work and coming home, same as always, so... no drugs or alcohol.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your personality, your ability to plan or organize tasks, or any new physical symptoms like headaches or weakness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My personality feels the same, and I can still plan out my day just fine. No headaches or weakness either. It's really just the talking thing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When others are speaking to you, do you find that you have any difficulty understanding what they are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble understanding other people. It's really just my own words getting all jumbled up when I try to talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually energized, irritable, or perhaps significantly down or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel pretty normal as far as my mood goes. I haven't had any big bursts of energy or felt super irritable or anything like that. And I haven't been feeling down or hopeless either. It's just... frustrating, you know? The main thing is just my speech getting all mixed up at work. But mood-wise, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol or recreational drugs recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't use any drugs at all. And as for alcohol... I mean, I might have a beer with dinner once in a blue moon, but nothing recently. It's definitely not that. I've just been going to work and coming home, same as always, so... no drugs or alcohol.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your personality, your ability to plan or organize tasks, or any new physical symptoms like headaches or weakness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My personality feels the same, and I can still plan out my day just fine. No headaches or weakness either. It's really just the talking thing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When others are speaking to you, do you find that you have any difficulty understanding what they are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble understanding other people. When my staff or the customers talk to me at the store, I know exactly what they mean. It makes perfect sense to me. It's really just... well, when I open my mouth to reply, that's when things get jumbled. But listening and understanding them? No, that part is completely fine.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking and your words get jumbled, are you aware in that moment that the words coming out are not the ones you intended to say?\n\nPatient: I don't really notice it right when it happens. To me, it feels like I'm saying exactly what I mean to say. I don't realize the words are wrong in that exact second. \n\nIt's usually... well, I'll see a confused look on the other person's face, or one of my cashiers will ask me what I'm talking about, and that's how I know it didn't come out right. But in my head, I thought I was making perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you find that you know exactly what word you want to use, but you simply cannot get the word to come out correctly, or do you find that your brain selects a completely different word than the one you intended?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, when I'm talking, it feels like I'm just saying what I want to say. I don't feel like I'm struggling to find a word, and I don't feel like my brain is picking the wrong one either. It just feels normal to me until I see that the other person doesn't understand. So I don't really know what's happening with the words themselves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to read written text, such as emails or signs at work?\n\nPatient: No, reading seems to be fine. I read the emails from corporate, look at the inventory sheets, read the signs... all of that makes sense to me just like it always has. I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I might have a beer with dinner once in a blue moon, but nothing recently. It's definitely not that. I've just been going to work and coming home, same as always, so... no drugs or alcohol.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your personality, your ability to plan or organize tasks, or any new physical symptoms like headaches or weakness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My personality feels the same, and I can still plan out my day just fine. No headaches or weakness either. It's really just the talking thing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When others are speaking to you, do you find that you have any difficulty understanding what they are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble understanding other people. When my staff or the customers talk to me at the store, I know exactly what they mean. It makes perfect sense to me. It's really just... well, when I open my mouth to reply, that's when things get jumbled. But listening and understanding them? No, that part is completely fine.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking and your words get jumbled, are you aware in that moment that the words coming out are not the ones you intended to say?\n\nPatient: I don't really notice it right when it happens. To me, it feels like I'm saying exactly what I mean to say. I don't realize the words are wrong in that exact second. \n\nIt's usually... well, I'll see a confused look on the other person's face, or one of my cashiers will ask me what I'm talking about, and that's how I know it didn't come out right. But in my head, I thought I was making perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you find that you know exactly what word you want to use, but you simply cannot get the word to come out correctly, or do you find that your brain selects a completely different word than the one you intended?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, when I'm talking, it feels like I'm just saying what I want to say. I don't feel like I'm struggling to find a word, and I don't feel like my brain is picking the wrong one either. It just feels normal to me until I see that the other person doesn't understand. So I don't really know what's happening with the words themselves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to read written text, such as emails or signs at work?\n\nPatient: No, reading seems to be fine. I read the emails from corporate, look at the inventory sheets, read the signs... all of that makes sense to me just like it always has. I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you ever find that your mouth, tongue, or lips feel clumsy or uncoordinated, or that your speech sounds slurred?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My mouth and tongue feel fine, they don't feel clumsy or anything. And nobody has mentioned that I sound slurred. It's just the words getting mixed up, like we were talking about. But physically, speaking feels completely normal.\n\nDoctor: When your words come out wrong, do you find that you are using words that sound similar to the ones you intended, or are you using completely different words that don't seem to make sense in the context of your sentence?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was saying earlier, I don't even hear the mistake happening myself. To me, it just sounds like the exact sentence I meant to say. I only know it came out wrong because the person I'm talking to looks confused. So I don't actually know what words they're hearing on their end—whether they sound similar to what I meant, or if they're just completely different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any numbness, tingling, or weakness in any part of your body, such as your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that. It's really just my own words getting all jumbled up when I try to talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually energized, irritable, or perhaps significantly down or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel pretty normal as far as my mood goes. I haven't had any big bursts of energy or felt super irritable or anything like that. And I haven't been feeling down or hopeless either. It's just... frustrating, you know? The main thing is just my speech getting all mixed up at work. But mood-wise, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol or recreational drugs recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't use any drugs at all. And as for alcohol... I mean, I might have a beer with dinner once in a blue moon, but nothing recently. It's definitely not that. I've just been going to work and coming home, same as always, so... no drugs or alcohol.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your personality, your ability to plan or organize tasks, or any new physical symptoms like headaches or weakness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My personality feels the same, and I can still plan out my day just fine. No headaches or weakness either. It's really just the talking thing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When others are speaking to you, do you find that you have any difficulty understanding what they are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble understanding other people. When my staff or the customers talk to me at the store, I know exactly what they mean. It makes perfect sense to me. It's really just... well, when I open my mouth to reply, that's when things get jumbled. But listening and understanding them? No, that part is completely fine.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking and your words get jumbled, are you aware in that moment that the words coming out are not the ones you intended to say?\n\nPatient: I don't really notice it right when it happens. To me, it feels like I'm saying exactly what I mean to say. I don't realize the words are wrong in that exact second. \n\nIt's usually... well, I'll see a confused look on the other person's face, or one of my cashiers will ask me what I'm talking about, and that's how I know it didn't come out right. But in my head, I thought I was making perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you find that you know exactly what word you want to use, but you simply cannot get the word to come out correctly, or do you find that your brain selects a completely different word than the one you intended?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, when I'm talking, it feels like I'm just saying what I want to say. I don't feel like I'm struggling to find a word, and I don't feel like my brain is picking the wrong one either. It just feels normal to me until I see that the other person doesn't understand. So I don't really know what's happening with the words themselves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to read written text, such as emails or signs at work?\n\nPatient: No, reading seems to be fine. I read the emails from corporate, look at the inventory sheets, read the signs... all of that makes sense to me just like it always has. I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you ever find that your mouth, tongue, or lips feel clumsy or uncoordinated, or that your speech sounds slurred?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My mouth and tongue feel fine, they don't feel clumsy or anything. And nobody has mentioned that I sound slurred. It's just the words getting mixed up, like we were talking about. But physically, speaking feels completely normal.\n\nDoctor: When your words come out wrong, do you find that you are using words that sound similar to the ones you intended, or are you using completely different words that don't seem to make sense in the context of your sentence?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. Like I was saying earlier, I don't even hear the mistake happening myself. To me, it just sounds like the exact sentence I meant to say. I only know it came out wrong because the person I'm talking to looks confused. So I don't actually know what words they're hearing on their end—whether they sound similar to what I meant, or if they're just completely different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any numbness, tingling, or weakness in any part of your body, such as your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that. My arms and legs feel fine. I haven't noticed any numbness or tingling, or any weakness anywhere. Physically, everything feels pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: When you see the confused look on someone's face, do they ever repeat back to you what they heard you say, so that you can see if you used a real word that didn't make sense or a word that isn't really a word at all?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Most of the time they just... they just ask me what I mean, or tell me they didn't catch that. They don't usually repeat the exact words back to me. So, I still don't really know if I'm using real words in the wrong place, or if it's just nonsense to them. They usually just look puzzled and wait for me to try saying it again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It is nice to meet you; what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Nice to meet you, Dr. Smith. Well, I came in because I've been having this issue at work lately. I supervise a retail floor, and... when I try to explain the inventory numbers or go over the weekend schedule with my team, my words just start jumping all over the place. \n\nI'll be trying to talk about the delivery trucks, and then somehow I'm suddenly talking about Thursdays, or the church grill, or the tone of voice my boss was using earlier. It just comes out that way. And people... they just look at me like they can't follow what I'm saying at all. It's making it really hard to do my job, so I figured I needed to get it checked out.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear sounds or voices that other people around you cannot hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I don't hear voices or sounds that aren't there. It's really just my own words getting all jumbled up when I try to talk to people.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually energized, irritable, or perhaps significantly down or hopeless lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I feel pretty normal as far as my mood goes. I haven't had any big bursts of energy or felt super irritable or anything like that. And I haven't been feeling down or hopeless either. It's just... frustrating, you know? The main thing is just my speech getting all mixed up at work. But mood-wise, I feel fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol or recreational drugs recently?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I don't use any drugs at all. And as for alcohol... I mean, I might have a beer with dinner once in a blue moon, but nothing recently. It's definitely not that. I've just been going to work and coming home, same as always, so... no drugs or alcohol.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your personality, your ability to plan or organize tasks, or any new physical symptoms like headaches or weakness?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. My personality feels the same, and I can still plan out my day just fine. No headaches or weakness either. It's really just the talking thing that's been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: When others are speaking to you, do you find that you have any difficulty understanding what they are saying?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have any trouble understanding other people. When my staff or the customers talk to me at the store, I know exactly what they mean. It makes perfect sense to me. It's really just... well, when I open my mouth to reply, that's when things get jumbled. But listening and understanding them? No, that part is completely fine.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking and your words get jumbled, are you aware in that moment that the words coming out are not the ones you intended to say?\n\nPatient: I don't really notice it right when it happens. To me, it feels like I'm saying exactly what I mean to say. I don't realize the words are wrong in that exact second. \n\nIt's usually... well, I'll see a confused look on the other person's face, or one of my cashiers will ask me what I'm talking about, and that's how I know it didn't come out right. But in my head, I thought I was making perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you find that you know exactly what word you want to use, but you simply cannot get the word to come out correctly, or do you find that your brain selects a completely different word than the one you intended?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, when I'm talking, it feels like I'm just saying what I want to say. I don't feel like I'm struggling to find a word, and I don't feel like my brain is picking the wrong one either. It just feels normal to me until I see that the other person doesn't understand. So I don't really know what's happening with the words themselves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to read written text, such as emails or signs at work?\n\nPatient: No, reading seems to be fine. I read the emails from corporate, look at the inventory sheets, read the signs... all of that makes sense to me just like it always has. I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you ever find that your mouth, tongue, or lips feel clumsy or uncoordinated, or that your speech sounds slurred?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. But physically, speaking feels completely normal.\n\nDoctor: When your words come out wrong, do you find that you are using words that sound similar to the ones you intended, or are you using completely different words that don't seem to make sense in the context of your sentence?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't tell you. I only know it came out wrong because the person I'm talking to looks confused. So I don't actually know what words they're hearing on their end—whether they sound similar to what I meant, or if they're just completely different.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any numbness, tingling, or weakness in any part of your body, such as your arms or legs?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any of that. My arms and legs feel fine. I haven't noticed any numbness or tingling, or any weakness anywhere. Most of the time they just... they just ask me what I mean, or tell me they didn't catch that. They don't usually repeat the exact words back to me. I don't think I use made-up words. They're all real words, but they definitely don't fit together the way they should. Actually, that's a big part of why I came in to see you.

It's just... frustrating, you know? The main thing is just my speech getting all mixed up at work. I don't realize the words are wrong in that exact second. \n\nIt's usually... well, I'll see a confused look on the other person's face, or one of my cashiers will ask me what I'm talking about, and that's how I know it didn't come out right. But in my head, I thought I was making perfect sense.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you find that you know exactly what word you want to use, but you simply cannot get the word to come out correctly, or do you find that your brain selects a completely different word than the one you intended?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that. Like I said, when I'm talking, it feels like I'm just saying what I want to say. I don't feel like I'm struggling to find a word, and I don't feel like my brain is picking the wrong one either. It just feels normal to me until I see that the other person doesn't understand. So I don't really know what's happening with the words themselves.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to read written text, such as emails or signs at work?\n\nPatient: No, reading seems to be fine. I haven't noticed any changes there.\n\nDoctor: When you are speaking, do you ever find that your mouth, tongue, or lips feel clumsy or uncoordinated, or that your speech sounds slurred?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My arms and legs feel fine. I haven't noticed any numbness or tingling, or any weakness anywhere. Physically, everything feels pretty normal.\n\nDoctor: When you see the confused look on someone's face, do they ever repeat back to you what they heard you say, so that you can see if you used a real word that didn't make sense or a word that isn't really a word at all?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Most of the time they just... they just ask me what I mean, or tell me they didn't catch that. They don't usually repeat the exact words back to me.
